# Lane coordination notes

Append a line; lanes read this file in a few hundred lines. Everything through Oct 21 is in docs/archive/lane-notes-through-oct-21.md (verbatim). Launch gates and their state: docs/launch-checklist.md. Staged send, rollback and freeze: docs/launch-runbook.md. Summary kept by Lane F, last refreshed Oct 24 2026 after e82 was live.

OWNERS: Lane B engine, checks and sweeps; Lane C pages and the ONLY lane that pushes (batched, FULL prePush once per batch); Lane D Tasks and sign-in; Lane E sweeps and audits only when assigned; Lane F this file, the checklist, the runbook and the Desk's reports to Sam; Lane S and Lane G closed until called.

STANDING RULES (full text in the archive, search the quoted title):
- Lock protocol: a lane writes a lock line (Lane, UTC time, what, tier) before a push, then the pushed and look-done lines, then the release line, in the format used by the entries below. No production read, even read only, while any lock is open.
- "THE TIERED PREPUSH RULE": FULL tier for engine and data files (scripts/engineDataFiles.mjs), FAST for the rest. Never push over a failing prePush.
- "STANDING RULE FOR EVERY LANE: one archive load at a time": no two lanes run prePush, a results table, a cold test or a chain at once.
- Efficiency rules (Sam, Oct 21): Lane C is the batch integrator, pushes at most once an hour; lane reports are three lines; Lane F reports only when something needs Sam or something landed.
- One engine (oneEngineAnswer), pages compute nothing. No secrets in chat, files or logs. No em or en dashes. Every report ends with a Search check line.

## FROM SAM, relayed verbatim by Lane F (10:59Z)

From Sam. Two engine fixes, Lane B, report what each changes before shipping.
1. ENTHUSIAST TERMS. "air cooled Porsche" returned all 167 Porsches. The resolver must understand common enthusiast group terms and filter to them, the same on Buy, Market Check, Sell and Tasks: "air-cooled" Porsche = 911 through 1998 (to the 993), 356, 912, 914, 930; "water-cooled" = 996 onward plus Boxster and Cayman; propose a short list of other common terms (for example impact bumper, long hood, G-body) with their exact definitions for my approval. If a term is not understood, say so plainly; never silently drop it.
2. MILEAGE NEVER MAKES THE ANSWER WORSE. 1963 Jaguar E-Type: 8 sales and a range; adding 36,000 miles cut the pool to 3, removed the range, and changed the years from 1962 to 1966 into 1964 to 1966. Rule: mileage narrows only if the narrowed pool still meets the range minimum; otherwise keep the original answer and add one plain line, "Too few sales near {miles} miles to narrow it. This is the range for all {n}." Mileage never changes the year window. Test on the 1963 E-Type, a 2014 911 and a car with deep sales.
One-engine rule applies. Three-line report. No em or en dashes.

## FROM SAM, relayed verbatim by Lane F (11:27Z)

From Sam to F. GoAskSam 2.0: one shell and four start pages. Lane C builds it, one lane, in stages, fast checks per stage and the full prePush once per stage. Attached: four reference images (Home, Find, Market Check, Sell: docs/goasksam-2-refs/01_Home.png, 02_Find.png, 03_Market_Check.png, 04_Sell.png) and the original brief. Where this block and the brief or images differ, this block wins. This is the way in, not the products: do not change any results page, card, engine answer or wording on results.

STAGE 1. SHELL AND RENAME
- One left rail on every page: GoAskSam wordmark; Home, Find, Market Check, Sell, Tasks (active item highlighted); RECENT SEARCHES (up to five real searches across Find, Market Check and Sell, each with a small product label; clicking restores that search and its refinements; nothing shown for a new visitor, never sample searches); then quiet links: For Business, The Daily Vroom (https://thedailyvroom.com), How Sam decides, About, Feedback, Privacy, Terms, Privacy choices. Remove the rail's "Keep looking" section: those are Tasks and live in Tasks.
- Sign in top right, or the existing account control when signed in.
- Rename Buy to Find everywhere user-facing. /find is canonical; /buy and every old /buy link 301 to the matching /find address with its query intact. Update the sitemap and canonicals. Results, cards and refinement bar unchanged.
- Rail tagline: "Enthusiast car market intelligence" (not collector-car).
- Report with desktop and 390px screenshots of a Find result and a Market Check result in the new shell.

STAGE 2. THE FOUR START PAGES (match the images, with these changes)
- Headlines in the existing serif, not the sans in the images. Same input component, size, microphone and submit on all four.
- Home (/): remove the redirect to /sell. "WELCOME TO GOASKSAM", "Find the car you're looking for.", "Tell Sam what you want. Search live enthusiast car auctions in your own words.", input with rotating examples, helper "Speak or type naturally.", then the two cards (Market Check: "See what similar cars have actually sold for."; Sell a car: "Discover where and how to sell."). No Daily Vroom section on the homepage (it stays in the rail). Submitting goes straight to populated Find results with the query run; never the empty Find page first.
- Find (/find, from the rail): "FIND · LIVE AUCTIONS", "What car are you looking for?", "Describe it naturally. Sam searches live enthusiast car auctions for you.", helper "Type it or say it. No complicated filters.", line under: "Live auctions across platforms. Real bids, real listings."
- Market Check: as the image ("What have cars like yours sold for?", "Real completed sales. A typical sold range. The evidence behind it.", VIN accepted). Arriving from a Find card shows results directly with the car carried over.
- Sell: build the start page on the NEW Sell (api/sellNext), not the old one. "Where should you sell your car?", "Tell Sam what you're selling. See what the auction data says about where and how to sell it.", "Tell me about your car, or paste a VIN...", line "Platform performance · Selling strategy · PowerSeller matching". STAGE 3. BEHAVIOUR
- Voice: the microphone works on all four (record with clear feedback, transcribe into the box as editable text, user submits). Graceful message on no permission or unsupported browser. No microphone shown where it cannot work.
- Value intent on Find: if a Find query contains sell, selling, worth, value or what's it worth, the Find results open with one line at the top: "Want to know what it's worth? See what {car} have sold for →" linking to Market Check with the car filled in. The search itself runs as normal. No other routing between products.
- Sign-in continuity: searching never needs sign-in. Saving a search or creating a Task signed out keeps the query, refinements and intended action through sign-in, returns to the same place, completes the action once (no duplicates) and confirms it. Cancelling keeps the search.

STAGE 4. SEARCH AND MOBILE
- Every start page's headline, subheading, card text and links are in the HTML on first load (server-rendered), not drawn afterwards in the browser.
- Each page has its own title and meta description; Home also carries Organization and WebSite structured data with a site search action pointing at /find. Add the new pages to searchCheck and the copy lint.
- Mobile: rail collapses to a menu, Sign in reachable, comfortable input and touch targets, no keyboard overlap, results layouts unchanged.
- Regression: every check in the brief's verification list, plus Sold before and Listed before, history popups and full-history pages, Market Check figures unchanged, Sell answers unchanged, Tasks separate from recent searches, anonymous search works, old /buy links work.

RULES: keep live wording on results ("Last bid seen", no "Sam's Take" on the range, "The typical range for cars like this."). No "collector-car" in new copy, no "AI" label, no first-person Sam, no source counts, no mention of other tools. One engine; the shell computes nothing. Three-line report per stage. No time estimates. No em or en dashes.

Branch laned-tasks3, stacked on batch 3 and 4 (setup questions). No SQL.
- WHAT "NEW" MEANS: a live car that matches the Task and is not in task.reported_ids (the last 500 ids reported for that Task) and is newer than the Task's checkpoint. A car reported once, in the opening update or any later one, is never reported again for that Task. Only after 500 further reports could an id fall off the list, and the checkpoint still keeps it out unless the listing itself is newer.
- Before this change the opening update's cars were never emailed (only the start email went). Now the opening update is queued (email_status queued, not for an edit of a running Task) and carries email_ids (every matched car, soonest end first) and email_cards (15 cards).
- lib/tasks/emailCaps.js is the one place: FIRST_EMAIL_CARS 5, LATER_EMAIL_CARS 6, and the "N more" sentence. digest.js reads the slim live rows (counts), reads full rows only for the cars shown, sorts by end time, caps, and passes the count and sentence to taskEmailBody. Every send still goes through io.send = sendTaskEmail (kill switch unchanged; nothing sends around it).
- Start email and generic update email: "Open the Task" link now #1A1A1A like Pause and Stop. (The Before it ends email in lib/live/buyAlerts.js still has its own red link, not changed.)
- Junk-folder line on the Tasks page: server fact notify.junk (lib/_email.js firstAlertPending): from a Task's start until the first alert email after the start email has been sent to the address.
- Page: compact Task rows (details), newest open, "N new" (t.unread), Pause and Stop; status line from limitFacts().statusLine; at the limit the limit card replaces the box (entryLine "Stop one to add another."). - HANDOFF TO LANE D (Lane B, Oct 25 2026): modelChoicesForBrand, branch names-e83, NOT pushed (Lane C takes it). The engine's model list for a brand, for the Task model question (replaces lib/tasks/tasks.js modelOptions for a brand with no model). IMPORT: `import { modelChoicesForBrand } from "../onebox.js";` (lib/onebox.js). CALL: `await modelChoicesForBrand(env, make, years)`. env = `{ supabaseUrl, supabaseKey }` (the engine's env, as oneEngineAnswer takes). make = the brand as typed or resolved ("Porsche", "Land Rover", "Mercedes-Benz"). years = optional `{ yearMin, yearMax }` (either may be null or missing): when given, the list holds only models sold in those years; if that leaves nothing it is the brand's whole list. Nothing else filters it (not colour, not what is on sale). RETURNS `{ make, options: [ { label, value, n, any } ... ] }`: up to SIX model entries, most-sold first (`label` and `value` are the model name as Find prints it, `n` = archive sales in the last 3 years, `any:false`), then ONE last entry `{ label: "Any Porsche", value: null, n: null, any: true }`. A brand with no models (unknown, read failed) returns just the Any entry. It reads the same list Find and Market Check offer for a bare brand (topModelsForMake: chassis codes collapsed, 996/997 are 911, base models), so Find and Tasks agree. Offline check scripts/modelChoicesCheck.mjs (in prePush). LIVE LISTS today (Find's own, first six): porsche 911 | Boxster | Cayenne | 356 | Cayman | 944. bmw 3 Series | 5 Series | M3 | 1 Series | 6 Series | 2002. land rover Defender | Range Rover | Discovery | Series | Defender 90 | Defender 110. ford Mustang | F Series | Bronco | Thunderbird | Model A | GT. mercedes-benz SL-Class | E-Class | S-Class | G-Class | C-Class | CLK-Class. The Tasks page is untouched.

  FIELD SHAPES (on the raw engine answer, on the mapped answer from mapEngineAnswer, and on the stored core from coreOf; both null when the answer has no pool or nothing clears):
  best_day = { day: "Sunday", count: 5, n: 13, measure: "median_vs_pool_median_pct", lead_pct: 29.3, median: 40200, pool_median: 31100, margin: 32.2, fence: { min_sales: 5, min_margin: 5 }, window: { months: 12, from: "YYYY-MM-DD", to: "YYYY-MM-DD" } }. Result tier only. Measure: the day's median price against the pool median, in percent. A day needs 5 sales; the winner sits above the pool median and leads the next ranked day by 5 points or more (margin is the real lead in points); fewer than two ranked days is null. dayBuckets is untouched.
  reserve_split = { winner: "reserve" | "no_reserve" | "none", a: { side: "no_reserve", n, median }, b: { side: "reserve", n, median }, n, gap_pct, scope_label: "2005 to 2012 911 Carrera", window: { months, from, to } }. Walks the page's own pool, then the generation (the spec's own year bounds, same trim), then the model in every year, no trim; stops at the first scope where both sides hold 8 sales; same window as the range and the same price fence. Cars with an unknown reserve flag are left out. winner is the side with the higher median when the gap (over the lower median) is 5 percent or more, else "none". Result and thin tiers; the walk reads the archive only on a result tier and only when the answer is under 6 s old, so a slow answer never waits for it.
  STAMP: stored answers key on the stamp, so e90 answers are stale after the bump and carry no fields until the full warm rewrites them; the warm must finish before a card can show either field. LIVE GOLDEN CARS (three per field: clears, walks, null) follow after the push and warm: I read candidates live on e91 and pin them in docs/golden-rules.md.

- NEEDED FROM LANE B (Lane C, 02:51Z), the 2006 BMW 325i Sedan card with no Sam block (listing 191227, BaT, 67,000 miles). CAUSE, not the Find key and not the whitelist: the card asks the engine for the same car you read, but through oneEngineAnswer, whose output gate refuses it. Your obdiag read runs runOneBox, which is before the gate. With gate=1 the same read shows tier result, 10 sales, 6,500 to 9,000, and the gate check receipts_carry_resolved_car FAILS: 5 of 10 cards are model "3-Series" titled 325xi ("2006 BMW 325xi Sedan"), against the resolved "BMW 325i". A pool where half the receipts are a different model name is read as a swapped pool, so the answer becomes a refusal with poolN 0 and no cards ("Sam could not verify this one"), and marketOf(refusal with no cards) is null, so Find draws no block. Market Check and Sell go through the same gate, so they should refuse this car too (to be confirmed after e93 when the ceiling lets me read). FIX (engine, yours): the gate should treat 325xi as the same car as 325i (a drive-train suffix on the same number: xi, i, d, ix), or the pool should count the 325xi as 3-Series siblings that stand. Also: a refusal that has a pool must carry it in thin_sales, because with poolN 0 the new thin_sales would say count 0 and the card would print "No car like this has sold at auction yet" for a car that has 10 sales. HOW MANY: of 139 live BMWs on a keyed Find search, 21 show no Sam block (97 range, 13 count, 7 not tracked, 1 pending); some of the 21 are other causes, I am sorting them.

- REASON LINE TABLE (Lane B, Oct 28, e99-20261028-trimfix, lib/reasonWords.js; every producer builds its line there, no page writes one; check scripts/reasonWordsCheck.mjs). Before and after, one per line, [id | before | after]:
    thin.house_share | BEFORE: The sales of this car are too one-sided to mark a range from. These are the sales on record. | AFTER: These sales come mostly from one place, so no range is shown.
    thin.few_sales | BEFORE: Not enough sales of this exact car to give a reliable range. These are the sales on record. | AFTER: Too few sales to show a range.
    thin.mixed_prices | BEFORE: These sales sit in very different price ranges, so no typical range is shown. These are the sales on record. | AFTER: These sales sit in very different price ranges, so no range is shown.
    thin.pooled_apart | BEFORE: These sales split into options that sit in different price ranges, so no one typical range is shown. Pick an option below to read its own sales. | AFTER: These sales split into options priced apart, so pick one to see its range.
    thin.few_comparable | BEFORE: Too few of these sales can be compared side by side to give a reliable range. These are the sales on record. | AFTER: Too few of these sales match closely enough to show a range.
    wide.mileage | BEFORE: Wide because 20,000 to 90,000 miles. Add yours. | AFTER: The range is wide because mileage runs from 20,000 to 90,000 miles.
    wide.year | BEFORE: Wide because these run from 2008 to 2014. Pick a year. | AFTER: The range is wide because the cars run from 2008 to 2014.
    wide.mods | BEFORE: Wide because 4 of the 12 sales are modified cars. | AFTER: The range is wide because 4 of the 12 sales are modified cars.
    wide.none | BEFORE: These sell on condition and history more than mileage. Here are the twelve sales. | AFTER: These sales vary with condition and history more than with mileage.
    year.note | BEFORE: No 2010 sales in this range. These are 2008 to 2009 cars. | AFTER: No 2010 sales here, only 2008 to 2009 cars.
    year.none_of | BEFORE: None of these sales is a 2010; they are 2008 to 2009 cars. | AFTER: None of these sales is a 2010, they are 2008 to 2009 cars.
    option.floor | BEFORE: Convertible, 5 sales, too few for a range | AFTER: Coupe, 12 sales, too few for a range
    option.range | BEFORE: Coupe $20,000 to $30,000, 12 sales | AFTER: Coupe $20,000 to $30,000, 12 sales
    options.note | BEFORE: Each option's count is read over its own window, so the counts can add up to more than the headline count. | AFTER: Option counts use their own time spans, so they can add up to more than the total.
    mileage.note | BEFORE: Too few sales near 40,000 miles to narrow it. This is the range for all twelve. | AFTER: Too few sales near 40,000 miles to narrow it, so this is the range for all 12 sales.
    chart.caption | BEFORE: By quarter, November 2023 to September 2026. Quarters with fewer than 5 sales are left out. | AFTER: By quarter, 2008 to 2009, quarters under 5 sales left out.
    chart.window | BEFORE: The chart covers 33 months, the range the last 11. | AFTER: The chart covers 33 months, the range the last 11.
    mileage.band | BEFORE: 12 sales within 15,000 miles of 40,000, since December 2025. The band reads the last 12 months. | AFTER: 12 sales 2008 to 2009, since December 2025.
    window.line | BEFORE: 12 sales, since December 2025. | AFTER: 12 sales, since December 2025.
    window.longer | BEFORE: Based on 9 sales over the last 24 months. | AFTER: Based on 12 sales over the last 24 months.
    basis.one | BEFORE: Based on one sale, in March 2026. | AFTER: Based on one sale, in March 2026.
    basis.since | BEFORE: Based on nine sales since March 2025. | AFTER: Based on 12 sales since March 2026.
    basis.latest | BEFORE: Based on nine sales, the latest in March 2026. | AFTER: Based on 12 sales, the latest in March 2026.
    basis.pool_one | BEFORE: Based on one sale of 1995 911 Carrera, in March 2026. | AFTER: Based on one sale of 1995 911 Carrera, in March 2026.
    basis.pool_since | BEFORE: Based on nine sales of 1995 911 Carrera since March 2025. | AFTER: Based on 12 sales of 1995 911 Carrera since March 2026.
    basis.pool_latest | BEFORE: Based on nine sales of 1995 911 Carrera, the latest in March 2026. | AFTER: Based on 12 sales of 1995 911 Carrera, the latest in March 2026.
    latest.sale | BEFORE: Latest sale: March 2026. | AFTER: Latest sale: March 2026.
    includes.from | BEFORE: Includes sales from 2021. | AFTER: Includes sales from 2021.
    scope.typed | BEFORE: Showing the Coupe as named. | AFTER: Showing the Coupe as named
    scope.every | BEFORE: Showing every edition of this car. | AFTER: Showing every edition of this car
    scope.same_price | BEFORE: The editions of this car sell for about the same, showing every edition. | AFTER: The editions of this car sell for about the same, showing every edition
    scope.only_kept | BEFORE: Only the Coupe has three or more sales of this car, showing Coupe. | AFTER: Only the Coupe has three or more sales of this car, showing Coupe
    scope.none_kept | BEFORE: No edition of this car has three sales on its own, showing every edition. | AFTER: No edition of this car has three sales on its own, showing every edition
    scope.no_year | BEFORE: No 1974 sales yet, showing 1973. | AFTER: No 2010 sales yet, showing 1972 to 1973
    scope.few_year | BEFORE: Too few 2006 sales, showing 2005 to 2007. | AFTER: Too few 2010 sales, showing 1972 to 1973
    refusal.verify | BEFORE: Sam could not verify this one, so no figures are shown. | AFTER: No figures are shown because this car could not be checked.
    refusal.not_a_car | BEFORE: (new in e98: no line) | AFTER: This does not read as a car, so no figures are shown.
    refusal.varied | BEFORE: Sales too spread out for a range. | AFTER: These sales are too spread out to show a range.
    refusal.read_failed | BEFORE: The archive read did not finish. Try the search again in a moment. | AFTER: This could not be read just now, try again in a moment.
    refusal.no_body | BEFORE: The 993 911 was not sold as a Targa. If you mean a different body or a related model, tell me which and I'll pull the right sales. | AFTER: The 993 911 was not sold as a Targa, try another body or a related model.
    refusal.not_tracked | BEFORE: We haven’t tracked a 1995 911 Carrera sale yet. When one sells, it’ll show up here. | AFTER: No sale of a 911 has been tracked yet.
    refusal.no_sales | BEFORE: No sales of a 1995 911 Carrera are on record in the last five years, so there is no range to show. | AFTER: No sales of a 1995 911 Carrera are on record in the last five years, so there is no range to show.
    refusal.builder | BEFORE: Singer cars are not compared with standard 911s, so Sam shows no range for this car. | AFTER: Singer cars are not compared with standard 911s, so no range is shown.
    cold.still | BEFORE: Sam is still reading the sales for this one. | AFTER: The sales for this one are still being read.
    cold.reading | BEFORE: Sam is reading the sales for this one. | AFTER: The sales for this one are being read.
    cold.missed | BEFORE: Sam couldn't read this one yet. | AFTER: This one could not be read yet.
    stale.line | BEFORE: Sales read on October 20. Sales since then are not in yet. Updating now. | AFTER: Sales read on October 20, newer sales are being added.

- EPOCH AND INGEST (Lane C, Oct 28): the sales epoch (app_config sales_epoch) has ONE writer, scripts/ingest.js, which bumps it whenever a run inserts rows (lib/_epoch.js bumpEpoch); a bump makes every stored Market Check row (MCWARM, stamped warmEpoch) read epoch_mismatch and recompute on a visitor's read, and ingest also deletes the stored rows of every make and model it touched. WHAT CAN RUN IT: (1) .github/workflows/nightly.yml ingest job, schedule 07:11 UTC = 12:11 AM PT Thursday, which is INSIDE the window between the Wednesday 5:05 PM warm and 7 AM PT and is NOT disabled (I may not edit nightly.yml); (2) the same workflow by manual workflow_dispatch (backfill_from/backfill_to); (3) nobody else: no other workflow calls scripts/ingest.js (nonsold.yml runs ingestAttempts.js, which does not bump the epoch), no vercel cron runs ingest (vercel.json crons: usageDashboard ops depth/fill, pullLive, refreshSpecLiveCounts, tasks, history photocheck, admin refresh), no ops task or button bumps it; a hand SQL write to app_config would. SO IT CAN FIRE: the Thursday 12:11 AM PT delta will move the epoch if it inserts any row (almost certain). SAM DECISION NEEDED: either disable the Nightly workflow in GitHub Actions for tonight and re-enable after 7 AM (then the epoch holds), or accept it; I have armed the Thursday pass as a FULL union re-warm at 4:52 AM PT (after the ingest) with epochMatchCheck --live before and after, so launch rows are on the live epoch either way. NEW CHECK scripts/epochMatchCheck.mjs (warmDay now reports epoch {current, checked, match, mismatch, noEpoch}); --live fails on any mismatch; it fails on origin/main (no epochMatch). RUN IT after the final warm and at 5 AM Thursday: it is wired into launchwarm.sh.

- FOR LANE E, matchedOption shape at ENGINE READY 6 (Lane B, Oct 28): an OBJECT, not a label and not an index. matchedOption = { dimension: "gearbox"|"body"|"edition"|"mileage", value, label, n, low, high, line, inHeadline, refine } or null when the title names no option. Print matchedOption.label for the name ("PDK") and matchedOption.line for the whole line; the matched option is also options[0] and optionLines[0] (the engine reorders them, so a page that prints the list in the order sent leads with it, and never searches for the match). inHeadline false means the match is a chip of another dimension: low, high and line are null, send matchedOption.refine to get its figures. Example, 2014 Porsche Boxster S PDK on the Find card: matchedOption {"dimension":"gearbox","value":"auto","label":"PDK","n":10,"low":32500,"high":35500,"line":"PDK $32,500 to $35,500, 10 sales","inHeadline":true,"refine":{"tx":"auto"}}, options [PDK, Manual], optionLines ["PDK $32,500 to $35,500, 10 sales","Manual $34,500 to $37,500, 10 sales"]. Fields: m.matchedOption, m.options, m.optionLines on the Find card; raw.matchedOption on a Market Check answer.
- MARK STALE, STAGE 1 (Lane B, Oct 28), before code. TODAY: scripts/ingest.js, after it inserts rows, reads every spec_market_cache key and DELETES each row whose key is a JSON array [make, model, ..., genCode] matching a touched make and model (or generation code): these are the Find, Buy and Tasks core rows. It does not touch MCWARM:* rows (Market Check, Sell, demos), LIVESPECS or QELIG rows (not JSON keys, skipped). It writes invalidated-specs.json for the caches job to recompute 60 of them; then bumps sales_epoch (only when rows were inserted). SERVE PATH: core rows (listingMarket, familyMarket, Tasks) go through prefetchSpecs, which trusts a table row as fresh because ingest deletes touched rows; it accepts only a row of this SPEC_V and engine stamp, under 24 hours old and from the same UTC day. COLD BEGINS in specCore (lib/live/search.js): no row means refreshSpec (a live ladder walk, the visitor waits) or, on the Find path, the pending "being read" answer with the walk behind it. Market Check, Sell and the partner demos read the MCWARM row, which ingest never deletes: an epoch or UTC-day mismatch is served stale as stored with the engine's staleLine and recomputed behind the response (waitUntil); that path already works and is not touched. MANUAL RUN: nightly.yml HAS workflow_dispatch (inputs backfill_from, backfill_to, sources, all optional). Blank inputs run exactly the nightly delta (node scripts/ingest.js --delta), then the caches job (VIN index, spec cache build, recompute of the list ingest wrote, examples, crossProductCheck); warm, attempts, premium and cube run on the schedule only, so a dispatch does not warm. Nothing for Sam to add to the file. Ingest spends OldCarsData requests as the nightly does. E99 ROWS SURVIVE UNCHANGED, NO RE-WARM, NO STAMP BUMP: the marker is a new optional field (market.staleSince) inside the existing market json; a row without it is fresh as today, SPEC_V and the stamp stay. DELIBERATELY STILL COLD (named): a row of another engine stamp or SPEC_V (the answer shape changed), a row over 24 hours old or from an earlier UTC day, and a stale "no sales on record" row (the claim can be wrong the moment sales land). Building now.

- VERIFICATION PLAN, SUPERVISED INGEST (Lane B; nothing here writes to production). Before Sam dispatches: (a) stalecount ops task, all pages: record rows, core, mcwarm, other, marked (expect marked 0 on the core rows), and keep the list of core keys; (b) warmDay ops task: record epoch.current and the MCWARM row count; (c) time one Find card read of a listing (buySearch enrich) as the baseline. Sam dispatches nightly.yml with all inputs blank (the nightly delta). After the ingest and caches jobs finish: (1) the Actions log line "spec_market_cache: marked N row(s) stale (.. already marked, 0 failed), 0 deleted" and "sales_epoch: bumped"; (2) stalecount again: rows and core not lower than before (ZERO DELETIONS: every core key from (a) is still there), marked about N, markedExamples are the touched makes and models; (3) pick 3 marked keys, read a listing of each through buySearch enrich: each answers a range card in under 1.5 s with stale true and the dated staleLine, none shows the being-read answer (three cars, one at a time); (4) 60 seconds later read one of them again: stale false, stalecount no longer lists its mark (the refresh landed); (5) one Market Check read of a touched bare nameplate: served stale with its staleLine, then fresh on the second read; (6) epochMatchCheck --live, expected to FAIL on MCWARM epoch mismatch until the next warm, rows checked equals (b). PASS = zero deletions, marked matches the log, three stale reads under 1.5 s with the engine line and no being-read answer, the refreshed row carries no mark, Market Check serves stale then fresh. FAIL = any core row missing, any being-read answer on a touched spec, a stale read over 1.5 s, or a mark still on a spec that was read twice.

- FOLLOW-UP 1, WHAT IS IN .claude/settings.local.json (Lane F, 17:38Z, read only, no values; local file, git-ignored, not in the repo; a copy of the same kind sits in the lane-a worktree). CORRECTION to the sweep line: it holds 13 JWT-shaped strings, 3 distinct, not three; and also 10 OldCarsData API key occurrences (one key, the sweep pattern missed that shape) and 2 tester-code link occurrences. All are pasted into Bash allow rules (env assignments and exports for one-off scripts).
- FOLLOW-UP 1a: all 3 JWTs are Supabase SERVICE ROLE keys (full database access, skips row security). Two are for the production project (ref otkmxyrglikdoychnmvy), issued 2026-04-09, expiry 2036-04-09, about six months old; one of those two has a misspelled issuer, so it is probably already invalid. The third is for a DIFFERENT project (ref mngqooxuafchlbcinotw), issued 2026-07-02, expiry 2036-07-02, about four months old. That ref appears nowhere in the repo or docs: Sam to say what that project is.
- FOLLOW-UP 1b, WHAT ROTATING BREAKS: the production service role key is read by every Vercel function, every GitHub Actions job (SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY secret) and the lanes' scripts. Rotating Supabase's JWT secret changes the service and the public (anon) key together; change both in Vercel and in the GitHub secret, redeploy once, and expect brief errors until both are set. Rotating the OldCarsData key means the Vercel variable, the GitHub secret OLDCARSDATA_API_KEY and the nightly ingest and warm. The other project's key breaks only whatever uses that project (not the product). The tester-code links die when CURTAIN_TESTER_CODE is rotated, which is already on the launch eve list. Nothing here is needed for the site to run: the allow rules can simply be deleted.
- FOLLOW-UP 1c, DISCLOSURE: while reading this file my own terminal output showed one OldCarsData key value once, in this session only; it is in no file or note. Treat that key as exposed to the session and rotate it with the others.
- FOLLOW-UP 2, HOW THE KEY TRAVELS (Lane F, 17:38Z, origin/main 48c8f680, for Lane D's stage 1 gate): (a) Vercel crons: no key in any path in vercel.json (none carries key, secret or token); Vercel adds Authorization: Bearer CRON_SECRET itself. Routes accept it through lib/_credential.js hasServerCredential or the cronOk-style compares listed in the sweep. (b) GitHub nightly (.github/workflows/nightly.yml, copy in docs/nightly-workflow.yml): the premium job sends PROBE_KEY as an x-ops-key HEADER, fed to curl from stdin (-H @-) so it is not in the process list. (c) scripts/warm.js sends x-probe-key as a HEADER, with warm:true as a plain body flag and an optional x-vercel-protection-bypass header; warmNamed.mjs, warmUnion.mjs and about 59 other scripts send x-ops-key (or x-probe-key) as a HEADER. (d) No script, workflow or lib sends the key in an address. (e) ONE sender puts it in a request BODY: scripts/buyTimes.mjs line 113 (action explain, field key), the caller of the buySearch b.key path. Nobody else calls the landing, finds, ladder or test send actions, so closing the body path only needs buyTimes.mjs changed to its header (it already sends x-ops-key on its page requests). keyInAddressCheck.mjs proves the server refuses addresses. The Bearer CRON_SECRET form is sent only by Vercel itself, never by a script.

- LIVE FEED SCOPE (Lane B, Oct 28), code 12ae5ec3 on lane-b-e96-fix, stamp UNCHANGED e99-20261028-trimfix, no stored answer changes, no warm, NOT pushed. CAUSE: pullLive stores every source OCD carries live with no scope; the only fence was isAbroadRow, which read the country CODE alone (country set and not US or CA). The Collecting Cars 2005 997 Carrera S in Presov has no country code on its row (its place is the text "Presov, Slovakia", currency EUR), so isAbroadRow said "not abroad" and Find drew it; the engine could not read it and the card printed the refusal line. FIX (feed level, one place): liveScopeOf (lib/live/search.js) uses the sales pool's own place rule (marketCountry: country code, then the state or city text, then currency, then venue) plus the excluded venues (Collecting Cars, Car & Classic, The Market, PistonHeads, out whatever the row says, even a US-placed one, by Sam's order); liveRows applies it once, so Find, Tasks, the chat searches, the landing counts and every header count agree; isAbroadRow is the same fence; the spec pages' live count reads the same fields (lib/specPages.js, a public page, number only). A row with no place on record stays in, as before (Sam Oct 25: no country stays in), and is counted apart. CANADA is in, as the sales pool has it. SWEEP: the count of live listings outside scope and five titles NOT POSTED YET: the warm is running (WARM START 16:55Z, no WARM DONE) so I do no production read; the read-only ops task livescope is written (live, out, byReason platform and country, bySource, unplacedKeptIn, examples) and runs first after WARM DONE and LOCK RELEASED. EMPTY SAM BOX: an in-scope car whose stored read holds nothing used to return null (an empty box); it now returns the engine's own no-sales line (noSalesLine: "No sales of a 2005 Porsche 911 Carrera S are on record in the last five years, so there is no range to show."). The engine fields a card prints for a car with no range: m.refusal_line (refusal, the table line "No figures are shown because this car could not be checked."), m.notTrackedLine (kind not_tracked), m.thinLine and thin_sales.reason_line (no range), m.line (still being read); a page prints one of these, never wording of its own. CHECK scripts/liveScopeCheck.mjs: 4 FAIL on the current feed, 0 with the fix (Slovakia, SK, GB and Car & Classic cars dropped; US, Canadian and unplaced cars stay; count equals cards; empty box gets the engine line). All 267 checks: the same 8 baseline failures, none new.

STAGE 3 the article cars on live e99 (Market Check lane; Buy, Sell and Tasks lanes agree unless noted): 2001 M3 17 sales $22,500 to $41,000 range; 2004 M3 49 sales no range (options split); 1994 911 Carrera Cabriolet 8 sales $58,000 to $94,500 range (one read had the Buy lane unanswered, the next read all four lanes agree); 1957 356 Speedster 10 sales $259,000 to $389,000 range (1956 to 1958); 2001 Diablo SE Coupe 1 sale, sales only; 2020 Speedtail refusal, no sales (Buy and Tasks listingMarket returned null: an empty box on the current code, fixed by the live scope commit 12ae5ec3, which returns the no-sales line); 2023 911 Carrera 11 sales $110,000 to $128,000 (2022 to 2024); 1969 Nova 8 sales $17,000 to $25,000 (1968 to 1970); K5 Blazer 1975 8 sales $24,500 to $37,000 exact; 2010 Murcielago 8 sales $397,000 to $551,000 but the pool is the 2008 to 2009 cars (the known year-excluded gap, after launch); 2023 C63 9 sales $83,500 to $106,000 (2021 to 2023); 1985 Defender 110 10 sales $23,500 to $53,000 (1984 to 1986); 1991 NSX 21 sales no range (gearbox split); 2005 Ford GT 24 sales $479,000 to $611,000 exact; 2002 996 Carrera 42 sales no range. No wrong model and no blank field on any. Also read: "1994 Porsche 911 Cabriolet" (no Carrera) answers 8 sales $58,000 to $94,500 on Market Check and Sell; Buy and Tasks read no stored row for that exact spec (a cold spec, the page waits and fills it). - TYPED EQUALS VIN GATE (Lane B, Oct 28), code cbc27d22 on lane-b-e96-fix, no stamp change, NOT pushed: ops task vintyped (read only: the stored VIN identities of the nightly rollout, the resolver and the stored Market Check rows; no visitor-path read, no address cap, no writes, no OldCarsData). It takes up to 50 stored identities, always the 996 when one is stored, and compares the identity a VIN page hands the engine with the typed name's resolution (lib/vinTyped.js): the same stored answer key, or the same tier, count, range, rung and options state. Returns checked, identical, differ, go (true only when every pair is identical), the first ten differences and the 996 flag. GO when differ is 0 and checked is 50; NO-GO otherwise, the differing pairs name the field. scripts/vinTypedCheck.mjs (own check, registered in prePush): the offline fixture of five real identities including two 996 shapes reads the same key typed and by identity; a pair with different keys and no stored answer, or a different count, is NO-GO and names the field; the check fails on the tree without the module. The live number comes from running the task after this push. All 268 checks: the same 8 baseline failures, none new.

- CHART LADDER, STAGE 1 AND THE FIELD SHAPE FOR LANE E (Lane B, Oct 28). STAGE 1: yes, it is computed at read time from the stored answer's own sales (rawPool, full rows with date and price) like matchedOption: NO stored answer change, NO stamp bump. One honest limit: the stored answer holds the window the answer reads (12, 24 or 36 months), not the wider 36 month chart set, so scoped candles need three candles of 5 sales of ONE option inside that window and will land less often than the pooled chart; dots land on everything else. Carrying the 36 month chart rows in the stored answer would land more candles but changes stored answers (a stamp decision, e100); I have NOT done it. FIELD SHAPE, on every result and thin answer as answer.chart (raw.chart), settled: chart = { type: "candles" | "dots" | "withheld", scope: null | { dimension, value, label, line }, size: "quarter"|"half-year"|"year"|null, spanLabel: string|null, caption: string|null, note: string|null, buckets: [{ period, label, low, high, typical, count }] | null, dots: [{ date: "YYYY-MM-DD", price: 12345, option: "PDK"|null }] | null, withheld: { reason: "too_few", floor: 3, n, line } | null, n }. candles: draw buckets, caption under it, scope.line above it when scope is set (the engine's words: "The candles show the PDK sales only, 24 of the 31."); note is the held-chart sentence ("The mix of cars changed from quarter to quarter, so a change here would not show the market moving.") and prints under the chart when present. dots: draw one dot per sale, date against price, option names each dot (null when the answer has no options), caption "Each dot is one sale, since December 2025.", no line between dots, no trend. withheld: draw nothing, print withheld.line ("Only 2 dated sales, too few to chart, so the sales themselves are listed."). Too few = under 3 dated sales. Pages print these fields and compute nothing; the old d.trend, d.chartCaption and d.trendHeld stay on the answer unchanged.

- ENGINE READY 10, FIELD SHAPE FOR LANE E (Lane B, Oct 28; the Find card rules, stacked on ENGINE READY 9, read ER10 wherever the job text says 9). SETTLED SHAPE, on every Find card as market.findAnswer (and the enrich action's sam_lines is exactly findAnswer.lines): findAnswer = { state: "range" | "few" | "split_unsaid", basis: "option" | "pooled" | "band", option: { dimension, value, label } | null, range: { low, high } | null, n, bid: "below" | "within" | "above" | null, lines: [string], pointer: string | null }. state range: n is 8 or more; print range as the headline, then lines in order; lines[0] is the count line ("17 sales since November 2024.") or, when the listing's own mileage band has 8 or more sales (basis band), "Ten sales near its miles."; lines[1] is the bid line ("The bid is below the range.") and is absent when the listing has no bid. state few: n is 1 to 7; range is null; lines = ["Seven Manual sales, too few for a range.", "See the sales for all seven."] and nothing else; pointer repeats the second line. state split_unsaid: the answer splits and the listing does not say which option; range null; lines = ["Sales of this car split into options priced apart, and the listing does not say which."] only. A Find card carries NO sale lines, no "pick one", no mileage band line unless the band has 8 or more sales and is the headline, never the listing's own mileage figure, never two ranges. Pages print findAnswer.range and findAnswer.lines and nothing of their own; option names the option the card answers for.

- THE MARKET ZERO STREAK (Lane B, Oct 28; one daycount read, archive only): NOT a feed fault, a real weekend gap. The Market sold 31 cars in the last 28 days, 1 to 3 on a selling day, and went to zero on Oct 16 to 18 and Oct 23 to 25 (Friday to Sunday both times), then 3 on Oct 26, 1 on Oct 27, 3 on Oct 28. The warning fires because zeroStreakFlag (lib/_ingestHealth.js) counts any run of 3 zero days in 14 for a source with sales in 28, with no weekday pattern, so any low volume source with a Friday to Sunday gap trips it. Pages that depend on The Market: none by name. Find, Tasks cards and the emails exclude it at the live feed (ER8 fence, liveScopeOf); its sold rows are 31 of the archive's sales in a month and join Market Check pools like any source. Other sources read in the same 28 days: Gooding, Mecum, Barrett-Jackson 0 sales; AutoHunter 0 (defunct, expected); RM Sotheby's last sale Oct 8, Bonhams Oct 11, Broad Arrow Oct 14 (event sources, they sell in bursts). Mecum, Barrett-Jackson and Gooding at 0 for 28 days could be calendar or could be the feed (the 12 later sources were never backfilled, see CLAUDE.md); I cannot tell which from the archive and say so. Check for the class (a source quiet because of its weekly shape versus quiet because the feed broke): not built, because The Market is not a fault; the proposal is zeroStreakFlag reading typicalByWeekday so it flags only a run longer than the source's own longest weekly gap. Say go and I build it.

The log agrees: after my enrich at 14:21:42 PT there is one cache_decision (spec_market_cache, key 996 coupe, hit true) and no read of the engine after it, where a refresh would log its rangeCache decisions. FIX (ab00ba8d, read time, no stored answer changes): the stale refresh has its own set; the warm waits for the refresh of a stale row and counts it warmed only when the fresh row is stored (before, a warm that met a marked row counted it warmed and left it stale). (1) scripts/crossProductCheck.js has its own cap: it reads a sample of 12 of its 88 specs a night (CROSS_MAX_SPECS, a window that moves 12 a day, so every spec is read in 8 nights; CROSS_MAX_SPECS=all reads all), stops starting work after 8 minutes (CROSS_MAX_MINUTES), has a hard stop 3 minutes after that, prints "CAP: read N of 88 specs (N not in tonight's sample, N skipped at the cap), N sub-check(s) skipped: names, N s. A cap is not a finding." and exits on findings only, never on a cap. (2) docs/nightly-workflow.yml (the copy of record) gives every one of its 41 steps a timeout-minutes and every job a limit its steps cannot add up past (caches: the cross-product step 13, VIN rollout 22, spec cache 17, VIN index 10, spec_pages 6, recompute 5, the rest 1 to 3; steps add up to 99 under a job limit of 110, so no step can run the job into the cancel). - WARM ON A PINNED ADDRESS, THREE ANSWERS (Lane C, Oct 28 18:12 PT). 1. YES for the address: warmNamed.mjs, warmUnion.mjs and postDeployWarm.mjs already take --base; I tested the deployment address of 99fa6e2b (the Ready build of 17:15:52, found with vercel inspect) with the same x-ops-key and bypass headers: warmHits answered HTTP 200 in 0.7 s with stamp e99-20261028-trimfix. A deployment address does not change when main is pushed. But launchwarm.sh itself curls goasksam.com for warmDemo, warmDay, staleleft and epochMatchCheck, so those need the same base. 2. PARTLY. The named part sends one call at a time with 4 specs, and the server runs those 4 one after another, so it is one spec at a time, not 4. More reads at once means more calls in parallel (3 calls of 4 = 12), which is a small change, but it also meets the rule of 3 heavy reads at once on a database that hit 97 percent CPU on Oct 25, and I cannot show zero errors for the first 50 reads without running them. The union already runs 3 shards at width 6 and warmUnion.mjs caps shards at 3. 3. YES, and small (read warmHits for the list first, warm only rows that are cold or stale), BUT it removes 0 of tonight's reads: I read all 149 named, golden, top and cross specs with warmHits at 18:08 and 0 were stored at the current epoch and stamp from before this run (the ingest bumped the epoch), 141 were stored by this run in its first 12 minutes, 1 stale, 7 not yet. The marked rows (301) the union already treats as not fresh. DECISION: the first part is 141 of 149 done after 12 minutes, the rest is mostly the union, which already runs in parallel; stopping would throw away nothing but gain no pace, and answer 2 is unproven. So I let tonight's warm run as it is. THE FIX FOR NEXT TIME, built in its own worktree and pushed after WARM DONE: the warm scripts take the pinned address from --base or WARM_BASE, refuse to start without one (exit 2) unless --live-main is given, launchwarm-pinned.sh passes it to every call, skip-stored in warmNamed, and warmBaseCheck.mjs (in prePush) fails if any warm script has goasksam.com as a default or fetches it. Proof it fails without the fix is posted with the push. Stalecount and pace every 15 minutes follow in this thread.

- COLD PATH, STAGES 1 AND 2 (Lane B, Oct 29, code 0c110203 on branch lane-b-e101-cold, based on origin/main 99fa6e2b, stamp UNCHANGED, no stored answer changes, no warm needed, NOT pushed). MARKET CHECK CAUSE FROM THE CODE (no production read yet; the warm is next): a spec with no stored row (never warmed, or stored under an older engine stamp, which reads as no row) is computed behind the first response: 8 s hand-off, then the computing answer, the compute kept alive by the platform until sellerDecision's maxDuration (90 s), a marker row (100 s) so asks start no second compute. The page asked again every 2 s for 45 s then drew the could-not line. A compute that needs 46 to 90 s therefore ended "still reading" on the page and was STORED seconds later with nobody told; a compute over 90 s is stopped by the platform and never stored (the next search starts it again). The refresh-marker fault I fixed in e100 is NOT this (it is the Find card stale refresh; a Market Check stale row is served at once). SECOND FINDING: every 2 s ask while the compute ran wrote one failed search_run row (failure_state still_reading), so the dashboard's 14 of 189 counts ASKS, not searches: one cold search could write up to 22. WHAT A VISITOR SAW: the loader steps for the first 8 s, then "The sales for this one are still being read." with moving dots up to 45 s, then the plain could-not line and Have Sam keep looking; the answer arrived on its own only if it landed before the 45 s; later it needed another search (instant, stored). FIX: COLD_WAIT_MS = 85,000 (maxDuration less 5 s) in api/sellerDecision.js, one constant for all three server literals, the Market Check page already takes the wait from the engine's waitMaxMs; an ask while the compute runs writes no row. Find, Tasks, the history hubs and the partner demos still hard-code 45 s in the page (api/buy.js, api/tasksPage.js, api/history.js, api/partnerDemo.js, api/_amazonDemo.js): the same class, NOT changed, Sam's call. NEW READ-ONLY OPS TASK coldruns (day's search_run rows grouped by search id: searches, still-reading searches, answered later, never answered, the list with typed text, rows, longest latency); I cannot list the 14 until it is live and the warm is done. CHECK scripts/coldWaitCheck.mjs (10 checks, registered in prePush): wait reaches the end of the compute's life, marker outlives it, one constant, the page's own loop on a fake clock draws an answer that lands at 20, 60 and 80 s, the could-not line only when nothing lands, no row per ask. WITHOUT THE FIX: 5 FAIL on plain main, and the page loop on main gives the could-not line for an answer landing at 60 s and at 80 s. Full suite: same baseline failures, none new. STILL UNKNOWN until reads after WARM DONE: the 14 searches (coldruns), the 1996 911 Carrera compute time, and whether tonight's warm covers the 1996 911 Carrera: it is in none of my nine or 61 (only a 1996 Boxster Spyder is); it is covered only if it is a named, golden or top-50-typed car or a live listing's spec (the union warm); warmHits on that list answers it. SELL (stage 2): Sell on a car with no stored answer calls the engine INLINE (lib/sell/sellBlock.js answerFor, one oneEngineAnswer call): no hand-off, no marker, no wait limit of its own, the same cold compute a Market Check hands off after 8 s, so 70.6 s is one cold compute (plus up to 4 s of block pieces, each capped at 2 s) and the 12 recorded searches' 9.1 s median / 31.6 s p95 is stored hits mixed with cold ones; the function has no maxDuration of its own in vercel.json. THE PAGE during that time: a moving bar with "Reading the sales for the {car}", after 20 s "This one is taking longer than usual" is added, and it keeps waiting with no cut-off (no could-not line, no hand-off). The answer is stored by the call, so the next Sell is fast. WARM COVERAGE OF SELL: warmDemo (named 19, golden, top 50, the nine, the 61) stores the answer AND the car-only Sell block; the union warm (warmLive) stores the answer only, so a Sell on a union-only car finds the stored answer and builds the block at the request (pieces capped 2 s). A Sell on a car with no row anywhere can still take as long as its cold compute. NOT DONE: a Sell hand-off (computing state and polling like Market Check), which is the class fix for Sell; I propose it after the launch unless Sam wants it first.

- EXAMPLES, CAUSE AND FIX (Lane B, Oct 29), code 36f5b081 on lane-b-e101-cold, stamp UNCHANGED, no stored answer change, NOT pushed. ANSWER TO YOUR QUESTION: both builders DO call the one engine (api/_historyData.js oneBoxFor -> oneEngineAnswer) and both ALSO had rules of their own on top of its answer, and that is the cause. marketCheckExample.reduce needed a pooled cluster and 5 cards; deskExample.buildOne needed tier result, a pooled cluster and 5 cards per generation, and 2 of 3 generations. Since e96 the engine answers a split or apart car with cluster null (the options as lines), so the builders rejected exactly the answers the engine gave: my count (the 2008 997 Carrera S 21 sales; Desk 997 31, 991 12, 992 13) was the engine's poolN and was true; "cluster null with 19 cards" is the engine's split state. The same pooled-cluster rule sat in oneBoxFor's stored-answer read (api/_historyData.js), so every hub and history page for a split car ignored its stored row and recomputed live (15 s race). FIX: lib/live/exampleAnswer.js engineFieldsOf stores the engine's own fields (state, cluster or null, count, lines, window, cards); the only gate is the engine's tier result; both builders use it; oneBoxFor reads a stored result that carries a cluster or options. Stored example shape: the old fields plus optionsState, headlineLines, optionLines, options, poolN, cluster nullable. FOR LANE E / LANE A (the pages that print them): the Desk page prints only label and soldCount per generation, the compare and the three sales, so it needs no change; the Market Check landing band: NO page reads the Market Check example row at all today (grep: only usageDashboard, the nightly script and a stale comment in api/marketCheck.js), so the band must print example.headlineLines when example.cluster is null, the range when it is not, from the stored fields, and compute nothing. OTHER BUILDERS SWEPT: Buy landing example (lib/live/buyExample.js) prints listingMarket's low, high and count as they come and needs kind range (the engine's own core kind), Sell landing example (lib/sell/sellExample.js) prints buildResult's pick and sales as they come; history and hub pages (api/history.js) read oneBoxFor and already draw option lines when cluster is null (optionLinesOf). No other builder has a cluster rule. CHECK scripts/exampleFromEngineCheck.mjs (alike, split and apart answers stored equal the engine's fields; no cluster rule or sales floor in either builder; oneBoxFor reads a stored split): on plain main 11 FAIL, and the old reduce returns null for a 19-card split answer. (2) THE ~765 SPECS: YES, BACK IN THE UNION FOR GOOD, as a LAST PASS: they serve no Find card, but a Market Check reader can type any of those cars and their stored row falls a whole epoch behind (served stale with a refresh behind, ER7) every day it is not warmed. scripts/warmUnion.mjs now runs a third pass with allScope=1 (task=warmLive &allScope=1: the specs of every live listing, in scope or not, never an ended lot), AFTER the in-scope passes so the in-scope specs are always done first; the command is unchanged (node scripts/warmUnion.mjs), --no-scopedout leaves the third pass off. The 4:52 AM pass is Lane C's read-only pass: it needs no change to read them; run staletexts kind=mc after it to see what is still behind. (3) THE SOTHEBY'S MOTORSPORT 2017 911 CARRERA 4S CABRIOLET, ended 181 minutes earlier, not marked ended. CAUSE: only the FULL walk (06:15, 14:45, 22:15 Pacific) marks a vanished listing ended (api/pullLive.js markVanished); the ending-soonest pulls (every 30 minutes, 09:00 to 14:30 Pacific) only refresh what they see; the 15 minute cron has NO pull from 14:45 to 22:15 Pacific. A lot that ends in the evening stays status live for up to 7.5 hours, and everything that reads live_listings counts it: the union warm, the header and landing counts, Tasks, the spec pages' live counts (Find already skipped it at the card, search.js "ended"). FIX (three parts): the ending pull marks over any lot whose end time passed more than 10 minutes ago and that the pull did not list again (feed.js markEndedPast; an extended auction is listed again and stays live); a quarter hour with NO pull marks over any lot ended more than an hour ago, database only, zero OCD (an auction extended past an hour is made live again by the next pull that lists it); liveRows never returns a lot whose end time has passed, whatever its status. - LAUNCH DASH AUDIT, STAGE 1 (Lane D, Oct 28, read from code on origin/main 510ce0eb, nothing changed): THE PAGE IS /admin (api/admin.js, titled GoAskSam numbers), not api/usageDashboard. Tabs and sections, one line each. LAUNCH (range buttons Today, Yesterday, Since launch, Last 7 days; the include-crew box; refreshes every 60 seconds): 1 answer rate per product (real, asked back, thin, error); 2 unmet demand (every thin or failed query word for word) and the top 20 cars asked; 3 drop-off (arrived, asked, real result, acted, came back) and the asked-back loop; 4 action (outbound clicks by platform and surface, Tasks created, paused, stopped, Task emails sent and failed, Sell started and finished, introductions, sign-ins, newsletter ticks); 5 came back and where from (source by first page); 6 right now (last 15 minutes: visitors, answers, failures); 7 speed (median and 95th, cold and warm, per product); 8 failures (busy line, 5xx by route, limits hit, searches failed, Held at the door, Held back, last 20); 9 cost per answer (Anthropic and OldCarsData credits by job); 10 feedback (a count of Feedback form messages); 11 where from and what on (country, state, device, near-me zips). BUY AND MARKET CHECK: searches and answers by day, what people did (refinements, receipts opened, clickouts, which-car questions, show more), range width against behaviour. DEMAND: top searches, unmet searches, searched-again cars (30 days). JOURNEY: recent journeys (24 hours), one journey by anon id, handoffs to Sell (7 days). RETENTION: weekly cohorts by person and by browser.
- LAUNCH DASH AUDIT, STAGE 1, THE ADDRESS AND THE KEY (Lane D, Oct 28): the address is goasksam.com/admin. It has NO dashboard key and no crew code. It answers the plain 404 page to everyone; the page then looks for the signed-in session in the browser (localStorage gas_auth_session) and only an account whose email is in the ADMIN_EMAILS environment variable sees the dashboard drawn in place. Every data call carries that person's sign-in token in a header, nothing in the address. So on his phone Sam: opens goasksam.com, signs in with the Google account whose email is in ADMIN_EMAILS (the sign-in bar on any page; while the site is held he enters the crew link once first, at goasksam.com/api/crew), then opens goasksam.com/admin in the same browser. USAGE_DASHBOARD_KEY is NOT read by anything any more (api/usageDashboard.js line 3 and 6488; LAUNCH EVE 8 deletes it), so rotating or deleting it changes nothing for Sam. The write-capable credentials are PROBE_KEY and OPS_KEY (the x-ops-key header our scripts and lanes use, which also read /api/admin parts check, door, refresh_now); rotating them locks out our scripts only, never Sam's phone. What can lock Sam out: (1) ADMIN_EMAILS not holding the address he signs in with (I cannot read Vercel env; look once tonight), (2) his sign-in session ending (fix: sign in again), (3) rotating Supabase's JWT secret, which signs everyone out (not on the list). After any rotation his steps are the same: open goasksam.com, sign in with Google, open goasksam.com/admin. The crew code rotation ends his crew cookie only; he re-enters the new code at goasksam.com/api/crew and the dashboard is unaffected. No key was printed.
- LAUNCH DASH AUDIT, STAGE 2 (Lane D, Oct 28, branch lane-d-launchaudit, not pushed): ALREADY ON THE PAGE: failures and 5xx by route (8), searches failed, busy line shown, limits hit, cold median and 95th per product (7), Task email failures (4 and Cost and Reliability), the Feedback count (10, for the picked range), Held at the door and Held back. MISSING, now added with recorded numbers only (lib/admin/health.js, wired in api/admin.js launch, rendered at the top of the Launch tab): (a) STATUS STRIP, GOOD, WATCH or ACT NOW, one sentence, a first step when there is something to do, and the last 60 minutes in numbers under it; (b) HOW FRESH: newest stored answer (the last warm), the sales epoch (app_config), Market Check and Buy and Tasks stored answers not stored today (UTC day, the launch warm rule, counts stop at 1,000), Market Check answers stamped with an older epoch; (c) CAPACITY, last 24 hours: 5xx, 504, limits hit, slowest search by product, errors by route and status; (d) FEEDBACK TODAY (Pacific day); (e) THE LAST REAL ANSWER per product (a range, bots out, 30 days). NOT RECORDED, and the page says so in those words: function timeouts (a function Vercel stops cannot log itself; read Vercel Logs), database and rate limit headroom (only limits hit and analytics writes lost are logged), replies to Task emails (they land in the feedback inbox; nothing counts them), per page latency (only search latency is recorded). THRESHOLDS, last 60 minutes, real visitors only (crew only if the box is ticked): ACT NOW at 5 or more 5xx, or 3 or more failed searches that are 20 percent or more of the searches, or 5 or more busy line shown, or 3 or more Task emails failed, or a cold 95th at 30 seconds or more; WATCH at 1 5xx, 1 failed search, 1 busy line, 5 limits hit, 1 Task email failed, a cold 95th at 20 seconds, a cold median at 8 seconds, or the newest stored answer older than 36 hours, or when the hour could not be read (the strip never says GOOD on a number it could not read); cold speed counts only with 5 or more cold answers; limits hit never make ACT NOW (a limit is the protection working). Cold limits sit above the recorded history (median about 3.9 s, 95th about 16.3 s, the One Box latency gate). Stored answers not stored today are shown but do not move the strip (they are all off-day each evening Pacific). launchHealthCheck 96 pass (fails on origin/main: no health file; with the unread hour mapped to GOOD it fails 6), launchDashCheck 153 pass, in prePush.
- LAUNCH DASH AUDIT, STAGE 3, THE DOOR (Lane D, Oct 28): the 20,026 held hits are 503s answered before any engine work (lib/_hold.js, the held_at_door event), 99 percent flagged bots, flat near 1,200 an hour (about 20 a minute across all bots), 20,019 of them on /api/history. Once the door opens those same bots reach real routes. Can they slow real answers? On /api/history, no at that volume: a page is cached at the edge for 120 seconds with 600 more served stale (a 404 for 300), so repeat URLs never reach the function, and the route already has its limit (history_page in lib/_ceilings.js, 60 a minute per device and 120 per address, 1,200 and 3,000 an hour, enforced by lib/_routeLimit.js, crew and our keyed jobs exempt, fail-open if the ledger is slow). What is NOT covered: one bot asking for many different URLs from many addresses is limited per address only, there is no whole-route cap, and the held events carry the route not the URL, so I cannot say how many of the 20,019 were distinct pages. Backstops outside the code: the Vercel firewall and Bot Protection decision (LAUNCH EVE 11). No limit number changed. THE HELD COUNT: it is a 503 with Retry-After counted as held_at_door, never an error (the Errors lines and the new strip read error events only; checked with 500 held visits changing nothing). The range total keeps earlier hits of the same day after the site opens, so I added the line that reads zero by itself: Held in the last hour, and Last held visit. Since launch starts at 7:00 AM Pacific and reads 0 from the first minute.

The strip does not call a limit or a still reading a failure: failure_state rate_limited, daily_limit and catching_breath are limits doing their job, still_reading gets its own line (WATCH at 3 or more and 10 percent of searches, ACT NOW at 10 and half), all other failed searches stay failures; a new failure_state in the catalog fails the check until it is classed.

- PRIORITY, THE NARROWED FOOTER (Lane B): CAUSE: my footer rule keyed on "the answer has options" (alike, split or apart), so the narrowed 2008 3-Series (one range with Manual and Automatic pills) lost its count and window. NOW the total and since date are dropped only when the card shows option BLOCKS (optionsState split or apart, more than one block); a range with pills (alike) is a single range card and prints its basis once. Every answer reached by a pill tap, typed miles or a band: alike keeps "N sales, since <window>." and the range sentence; split or apart prints "Updated <date>." and the link only. APPLIED FILTERS (engine fields, FOR LANE E): appliedFilters [{ kind (mileage, gearbox, body, trim, variant, year, history), words, clear }] and appliedLine ("Narrowed to 50,000 to 100,000 miles and Manual." or "Narrowed to within 5,000 miles of 60,000."); clear is the refine to send to take THAT filter off. scopeLine now carries it ("Convertibles, coupes and sedans · Narrowed to 50,000 to 100,000 miles · latest sale Oct 16, 2026") and metaLine (the all-sales caption) is the same line. - CACHE HEADER REPORT, NOTHING CHANGED (Lane C, Oct 29 ~3:05 AM PT, read from the repo and curl -I on the bee760b6 pinned build): (1) STATIC CODE FILES (/js/onebox.js, /js/auth.js, /styles.css, every file in public/): "public, max-age=0, must-revalidate" with an ETag. A browser keeps a copy but must ask Vercel each time; unchanged gives a 304, changed gives the new file. That is safe, it is the Vercel default for public/ and vercel.json sets no override (its only headers block is security headers). The Vercel edge copy (x-vercel-cache HIT, age about an hour) is replaced on every deploy. So a normal reload gets new code from these. (2) THE PAGES (HTML, built by api/*.js): crew, crew-cookie, key, sealed and pre-launch requests get "private, no-store". Once the site is launched, public requests get "public, s-maxage=30" (Market Check, Tasks, spec page), BUT Find (api/buy.js:177) gets s-maxage=600 when not "late" (10 minutes at the edge), /sell next (api/sellNext.js:81) gets "max-age=0, s-maxage=600, stale-while-revalidate=3600", and the spec page route (api/specPage.js:87) gets "s-maxage=3600, stale-while-revalidate=86400" (a day of stale). (3) MOST LIKELY CAUSE of "old layout until hard refresh": pages that carry their own CSS and JS inline (Feedback, About, Tasks, the shell) have the layout IN the HTML; a browser or edge copy of that HTML is old until it expires or is revalidated, while the external /js and /styles files revalidate every time, so HTML and external files can come from two different builds for up to the s-maxage above, and the page JS can run against old markup. I did not reproduce Sam's case (pre-launch every page is no-store for crew), so this is the code path, not a measured fact. SMALLEST SAFE FIX (needs Sam's yes, nothing changed): after launch cap Find and sell-next at s-maxage=30 like the other pages and drop the day-long stale-while-revalidate on the spec page to 300; optionally add a build id to the external /js and /styles URLs so HTML and files always come from one build. Pre-launch no change is needed.

- HOLDING PAGE AT ZERO (Lane G, Oct 28, page code only, answers Sam's question), branch lane-g-holdzero code 2ecfc25c on origin/main e01be66d, NOT pushed, FULL prePush not run by me. WHAT IT DID (read in lib/_hold.js): (1) The time is exact: LAUNCH_AT 2026-10-29T07:00:00-07:00 = 7:00:00 AM Pacific (14:00 UTC; PDT until Nov 1). (2) At and after zero it showed the headline and footer only: the timer and the "Opening ..." line were deleted, no negative number, but no sign of what was happening. (3) It never refreshed itself: no poll, no reload, no redirect, so a reader who arrived at 6:55 stayed on the holding page after the seal was lifted until they reloaded by hand. THE FIX (lib/_hold.js holdingHtml, the one page every held route answers): at zero (client clock, or server render after zero) the same headline and look with a calm "Opening now" line, no clock; from page load it asks "/" every 15 to 25 seconds (random spread so readers do not arrive together; also on return to the tab; fetch with no-store, redirect manual) and goes to the home page (location.replace("/")) as soon as that answer is no longer 503, so it works whether the seal is lifted at 7:00 or minutes later. The seal itself is unchanged: opening is still CURTAIN_SEALED off plus the redeploy, the page never opens the site, and it never computes a market fact. EVERY OTHER PAGE IT AFFECTS: every held route (Home, Find, Market Check, Sell, Tasks, For business, spec, history, shared result) renders this one holdingHtml, so all get it; the calm error page (calmHtml, "One moment") is a different state with no countdown and is not changed.
